The methods like `Remove`, `SetError` have pointer receivers, so I need a pointer.\n\nLooking at the existing test code, `createFS` returns a value but Go automatically takes the address when calling pointer receiver methods on addressable variables. Still, returning a pointer from `setupIntegrationTest` makes the code cleaner and more explicit. The `FakeFS` struct wraps `fstest.MapFS` and has pointer receiver methods for operations like `Remove` and `SetError`. These are pointer receiver methods, so I need to pass a pointer to the FakeFS. The existing code does `storagetest.Register(schema, &fs)` with a pointer, but that's passing the address of a local variable—when the function returns, that would be a dangling pointer... unless Go's escape analysis kicks in and allocates it on the heap instead. But then `fs` gets copied to the return value, so the pointer stored in `Register` and the returned `fsys` would be different copies, which means methods on `fsys` wouldn't affect what `Register` has stored. Yet the tests pass, so `Register` must be storing a factory function rather than the `FakeFS` directly. I'm looking at how the test uses `fsys` after it's returned from `createFS`, and I realize there's a critical issue: if `Register` is storing a pointer to the stack-allocated `fs`, but `createFS` returns a copy of that value, then modifications to `fsys` in the test wouldn't actually affect the registered storage backend.\n\nHowever, the key insight is that `fstest.MapFS` is itself a map type, so when you copy the `FakeFS` struct, the map header gets copied but both copies end up pointing to the same underlying hash table. This means modifications through `fsys` are actually visible through the pointer that `Register` stored, which is why the tests work despite the apparent copy.\n\nFor my integration tests, I should follow the same pattern: create a `FakeFS`, call `SetFiles` on it, register it with a pointer, and then return that same pointer from my setup function so any modifications I make in tests are guaranteed to be reflected in the registered instance.\n\nSetting up the integration test function...\n\nI should also verify that `squirrel.Eq` works as a filter for artist queries, which it does based on the existing test patterns.\n\nFor albums, the filter uses the table-prefixed format like `\"album.name\"`, and albums have an `AlbumArtistID` field along with a `Participants` collection that I can use to verify the test expectations. Package scanner_test contains integration tests for the Navidrome media scanner.\n// These tests exercise the full scanning pipeline: file discovery → metadata\n// extraction → DB persistence, using (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\t\"testing\"\n\t\"testing/fstest\"\n\n\t\"github.com/Masterminds/squirrel\"\n\t\"github.com/navidrome/navidrome/conf\"\n\t\"github.com/navidrome/navidrome/conf/configtest\"\n\t\"github.com/navidrome/navidrome/core\"\n\t\"github.com/navidrome/navidrome/core/artwork\"\n\t\"github.com/navidrome/navidrome/core/metrics\"\n\t\"github.com/navidrome/navidrome/core/storage/storagetest\"\n\t\"github.com/navidrome/navidrome/db\"\n\t\"github.com/navidrome/navidrome/model\"\n\t\"github.com/navidrome/navidrome/model/request\"\n\t\"github.com/navidrome/navidrome/persistence\"\n\t\"github.com/navidrome/navidrome/scanner\"\n\t\"github.com/navidrome/navidrome/server/events\"\n\t\"github.com/navidrome/navidrome/tests\"\n)\n\n// integrationEnv bundles the objects needed by each integration test.\ntype integrationEnv struct {\n\tctx context.Context\n\tds  *tests.MockDataStore\n\ts   model.Scanner\n\tlib model.Library\n}\n\n// setupIntegrationEnv creates a fresh, isolated test environment:\n//   - loads application config (once, via sync.Once in tests.Init)\n//   - saves and later restores conf.Server so each test is independent\n//   - registers a fake filesystem under the given schema name\n//   - initialises the shared in-memory SQLite schema via db.Init\n//   - creates an admin user and a library record\n//   - registers a t.Cleanup that wipes the schema after the test\n//\n// The returned *storagetest.FakeFS is the live filesystem object; callers can\n// call Add / Remove / Move / SetError on it to mutate the virtual filesystem\n// between scanner runs.\nfunc setupIntegrationEnv(t *testing.T, schema string, files fstest.MapFS) (*integrationEnv, *storagetest.FakeFS) {\n\tt.Helper()\n\n\t// Load base config (DbPath = file::memory:?cache=shared, from tests/navidrome-test.toml).\n\t// sync.Once ensures this only really executes once per test binary.\n\ttests.Init(t, false)\n\n\t// Snapshot conf.Server now and restore it when the test finishes.\n\tt.Cleanup(configtest.SetupConfig())\n\n\t// Point the scanner at our fake storage scheme.\n\tconf.Server.MusicFolder = schema + \":///music\"\n\tconf.Server.DevExternalScanner = false\n\n\t// Build the fake filesystem and register it for the chosen scheme.\n\tfakeFS := &storagetest.FakeFS{}\n\tfakeFS.SetFiles(files)\n\tstoragetest.Register(schema, fakeFS)\n\n\t// Context carries the admin user identity expected by scanner internals.\n\tctx := request.WithUser(context.Background(), model.User{ID: \"123\", IsAdmin: true})\n\n\t// Ensure the DB schema exists (migrations are idempotent).\n\tdb.Init(ctx)\n\n\t// Tear down: wipe all tables after the test so the next test starts clean.\n\tt.Cleanup(func() {\n\t\tif err := tests.ClearDB(); err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Logf(\"ClearDB: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t})\n\n\t// Wire up the real persistence layer against the shared in-memory DB.\n\tds := &tests.MockDataStore{RealDS: persistence.New(db.Db())}\n\n\t// The scanner requires an admin user to exist in the DB.\n\tadminUser := model.User{\n\t\tID:          \"123\",\n\t\tUserName:    \"admin\",\n\t\tName:        \"Admin User\",\n\t\tIsAdmin:     true,\n\t\tNewPassword: \"password\",\n\t}\n\tif err := ds.User(ctx).Put(&adminUser); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"create admin user: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Create the scanner under test.\n\ts := scanner.New(ctx, ds, artwork.NoopCacheWarmer(), events.NoopBroker(),\n\t\tcore.NewPlaylists(ds), metrics.NewNoopInstance())\n\n\t// Create the single library that points at the fake storage.\n\tlib := model.Library{\n\t\tID:   1,\n\t\tName: \"Integration Test Library\",\n\t\tPath: schema + \":///music\",\n\t}\n\tif err := ds.Library(ctx).Put(&lib); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"create library: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\treturn &integrationEnv{ctx: ctx, ds: ds, s: s, lib: lib}, fakeFS\n}\n\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// Test 1 – Full scan: file discovery, album creation, folder creation\n// in short mode\")\n\t}\n\n\trevolver := storagetest.Template(map[string]any{\n\t\t\"albumartist\": \"The Beatles\",\n\t\t\"album\":       \"Revolver\",\n\t\t\"year\":        1966,\n\t})\n\n\tenv, _ := revolver(storagetest.Track(2, \"Eleanor Rigby\")),\n\t\t\"The Beatles/Revolver/03 - Love You To.mp3\":   revolver(storagetest.Track(3, \"Love You To\")),\n\t})\n\n\t// ── Stage: full scan ──────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\t_, err := env.s.ScanAll(env.ctx, true)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"ScanAll: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// ── Stage: file persistence {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"MediaFile count: want 3, got %d\", got)\n\t}\n\n\t// ── Stage: album persistence ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\talbums, err :=
